Boats arriving fromT pay full inward dues if discharging at Sbasi town, local trade dues if dischargmg near the West station; but in t table they are all entered as local traders. In the same way boats fro Shasi town to Tangyang, pay full ontad us but are classed among local clearances. Boats arriving from Tangyang for other ports pay full outward dues. They are entered in the Shipping table irst as local entries, then as interport clearances. Boats arriving from other parts for Tangyang have usually no dues to pay, but in the Shipping table they are entered, firstly, as interport entries and, secondly, local clearances. 11.-1M POR TS. 1o. 290 CUSTOMSGAErTE. Coo.-Do., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 517. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. CONFUOIUS CHANNEL. NOTIE is hereby given that, owing to the extension of the South-east Spit, the Dove&#39;s Nest Lower Buoy has been shifted 0. 15 of a mile W.S.W. (magnetic) of its former position. SeaGHAI, 4th October 1911. No. 518. KIUNGCHOW DISTRICT. HAINAN STRAIT. RuEr REPORTEa) IN WESTERN APPROACH. NOTICE is hereby given that, according to British Notice to Mariners No. 1,348, the existence of a reef (depth of water not stated), bearing N. 70* W., distant 61 miles from Lamko Lighthouse, has been reported. SHANGHAI, 24th November 1911.
1911. NOTIFICATIONS 291 No. 519. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. LANGSHAN CROSSING. ALTERATION IN COLOUR OF UPPER CROSSING Buoy. NoTIo is hereby given that the Johnston Flats have extended to the eastward and that the Upper Crossing Buoy now lies ij cables off the 3-fathom contour of that bank. Vessels should therefore pass to the eastward of the buoy. The buoy will be altered in colour from red and black horizontal stripes to black. The channel to the eastward of the buoy isn mile wide. SoANGHAI, 2nd December 1911. No. 520. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. LANGSHAN OBOSSING. ALTERATION IN BUOYAGE. NoTICE is hereby given that, on or about the 16th instant, the Vine Point Buoy and the Waterman Bank Upper Buoy will be shifted, owing to changes in the channel. The Vine Point Buoy will be moored in 30 feet of water at low water of spring tides, and from the buoy Langshan Pagoda will bear N. 47* E., distant 8.42 miles. The Waterman Bank Upper Buoy will be moored in 4o feet of water at low water of spring tides, and from the buoy Langshan Pagoda will bear N. 53W E., distant 7.92 miles. The characteristics of these buoys remain unchanged. All bearings given are magnetic. SHANGHAI, 15th December 1911. &amp;gt;4fn
